`edu-no-drug-free-addendum-rule-oh` now state both limbs, and both warn against the opposite drafting
error as well. One ambiguity is stated rather than resolved, per Part 5: (A)(6)(a)(i) reads as a
deeming provision ("a landlord has ... if"), not expressly as the only route, and no case law was
read. The existing row `edu-mandatory-drug-termination-oh` has the same defect and is reported in §6.

- **Ten rows stated an absence or a coverage flatly in the body.** Rule 19 requires the corpus to be
named in the body, not only the notes, and each of these had the bound in its notes alone:
`edu-limitation-of-actions-oh`, `edu-dv-confidentiality-oh`, `edu-utility-shutoff-regime-oh`,
`edu-binding-arbitration-oh`, `edu-family-child-care-oh`, `edu-purpose-limitation-oh`,
`edu-tenant-screening-oh`, `edu-written-notice-required-oh`, `edu-optional-lease-terms-oh`,
`edu-dv-protection-order-not-moved-oh`. Each body now names exactly how far its search went — which
for five of them means saying that the chapter the row depends on is **outside** this library's Ohio
corpus and was fetched section by section (chs. 2305, 4933 with 4901:1-18, 2711, 5104, 3113). **No
legal conclusion changed.**

- **A case-law proposition asserted as statute.** `edu-no-holdover-formula-oh` said accepting rent
after serving a notice "waives that notice **as a matter of law, however your lease is worded**".
`ohsearch find "acceptance of rent"` returns **zero** sections across the 37 held chapters, `waive`
returns only R.C. 5321.13, R.C. 5321.18 and an unrelated national-guard section, and no case law was
read for the row. Both that row and `edu-no-rent-demand-bar-oh` now attribute the rule to the
existing `edu-waiver-by-acceptance-oh`, which carries the cases, and label it case law. That row's
own basis raises a library-level question, reported in §7.

- **A note that counted five and listed four.** `edu-statutory-early-termination-oh` named
R.C. 5321.03's "five grounds" and then listed four, dropping the very (A)(5) registered-offender
ground that an earlier correction in this pass had been about. All five are now listed with their
division numbers.

- **A fee stacked on full compensation with no measure.** `alarm-tampering-fee-oh` charged
`{{alarm_tampering_fee}}` "in addition to the actual cost of repairing or replacing the alarm",
justified only as "Landlord's charge for Tenant's breach ... not a penalty set by law" — which names
no loss at all. Since R.C. 5321.14 unconscionability is Ohio's only ceiling here, that is the shape
most exposed to it. The charge is now tied to a named loss the repair cost does not reach
(inspection, the attendance to restore, re-testing) and recited as a reasonable estimate — the same
discipline this pass required of the holdover figure.

- **"Whichever is greater" dropped from R.C. 5321.16(A).** Two rows stated the deposit-interest
threshold as "fifty dollars or one month's rent", which reverses the rule on any ordinary rent: on a
one-thousand-dollar rent interest runs on the excess over a month's rent, not over fifty dollars. A
sweep of every row mentioning "fifty dollars" or a five-per-cent figure found **two more** with the
same omission. All four now carry the whole test, including that interest runs on the **excess** only
and is computed and paid annually: `edu-furnishings-included-oh`, `edu-no-waterbed-rule-oh`,
`edu-deposit-last-month-rent-oh`, `edu-no-security-deposit-holding-rule-oh`.

- **Four citations to sections the pass never read, unmarked in the body** (rule 79):
R.C. 3742.37 and 3742.39 in `edu-lead-safe-certification-oh`, R.C. 3749.07 in `edu-pool-safety-oh`,
R.C. 3791.04 in `edu-security-devices-oh`. Each is now marked as unread where it is cited, with what
the reader should go and check. A fifth, R.C. 5311.27 in `edu-conversion-notice-oh`, was already
marked and needed nothing.

- **An unsourced proposition in a builder note.** `tenant-portal-oh`'s bracketed note asserted that
"an Ohio tenant has a statutory right to refuse to conduct further transactions electronically that
this Lease cannot waive". Chapter 1306, Ohio's UETA, is outside the corpus and was not read for the
clause. The note now attributes the proposition to the library's own
`edu-electronic-notice-regime-oh`, says the chapter was not read, and explains that the Lease grants
the right outright so the clause is correct either way. No drafting changed.

- **Bookkeeping, corrected in §2 and in §4's closing decision.** Eight existing rows carry OH, not
six; the delta holds thirty-seven clauses, not thirty-two; the delta keys 253 rows to topics rather
than 257 because the four ALREADY_ANSWERED topics add no row. The full reconciliation is printed
under §2 so the four numbers cannot be misread again. §1's fetched-section count also came from a
regex that was picking subdivision markers out of headings as if they were section numbers; it is
fixed, and the list now reads 49 with the two chapter-level entries called out as chapters.

#### One further error, found by the lead while verifying a round-1 fix

Round 1's bound-naming finding sent the lead back to `edu-limitation-of-actions-oh` to write its
search bound into the body. Checking that the new sentence was true turned up a real error in the
row, and in the batch agent's reasoning behind it.

The row opened: "Nothing in Ohio's landlord-tenant or eviction chapters sets a deadline for suing."
**R.C. 1923.01(B), re-read section-open (Effective: April 9, 2025; Latest Legislation Senate Bill 237
- 135th General Assembly): 'An action shall be brought under this chapter within two years after the
cause of action accrues.'** That is a two-year limit on every Chapter 1923 action — the eviction
itself — and it is 2025 law, so a form or guide written earlier will not carry it.

Two things make this worth recording rather than just fixing. First, **the agent found the section
and reasoned past it**: its battery note says the cross-search "returns only R.C. 1923.01 out of
chapters 5321 and 1923 and that is not a limitation period", and the same note lists `within two
years` with **nine hits** among its positive controls without any of the nine being inspected. A
positive control run and not read is how a provision sitting in the corpus the whole time got counted
as evidence of its own absence. Second, **the delta already contradicted itself**:
`edu-eviction-process-oh`, written in a different batch, states the two-year period correctly and
cites R.C. 1923.01(B). Two of this pass's own rows disagreed about a period that decides whether a
landlord may file at all, and nothing in the per-topic workflow would have caught it, because each
row was correct against its own research.

Chapter 5321 was re-checked and does carry no limitation period, so that half of the original claim
stands. The row now leads with R.C. 1923.01(B), dates it to S.B. 237, points to the procedure row,
and adds the two-year filing step to its safe-practice sentence.

**The cross-check for the same shape, run over the whole pass, came back clean.** Two sweeps:

- Every term in every confirmed-absence battery was re-run and its hits inside chapters 5321 and
1923 compared against what the row's notes name. **75 terms** have a core-chapter hit the notes do
not name — but every one is a *positive* control doing its job (`deposit` returning 396 sections,
`landlord` returning six sections of chapter 1923, and so on), and a positive is not required to
inventory its hits. The two sections that recur across several rows were opened and are benign in
every case: **R.C. 5321.051** matches `registration` only through "state registry" and
"registration-exempt" in the sex-offender eviction provision, and **R.C. 5321.20** is the rent-control
policy-findings section, whose prose catches `allocation`, `employment`, `political`, `property tax`,
`condominium` and `declaration` without being about any of them. **No control term returns a
core-chapter hit at all.**
- The sharper sweep went after the actual failure: a hit the agent *looked at and dismissed in prose*.
Searching every confirmed-absence row's notes for dismissal language next to a named chapter 5321 or
1923 section found **seven** such dismissals. Six are reasoned correctly and verifiable — the
`demand` hits in those two chapters really are three jury demands plus R.C. 5321.04(B)'s "repeated
demands for entry"; R.C. 5321.02(A)(1) to (A)(3) really are a code complaint, a landlord complaint
and collective bargaining, and none is a police call. The seventh was the one above.

So the error was a single instance of its kind rather than a pattern, but it is the kind that per-topic
research cannot catch, because each row was right against its own search. That is why the SOP proposal
below asks for a cross-row consistency check rather than only a per-row one.

#### Round 2 — twelve findings, eleven of them errors, all fixed

Round 2 re-checked the **36 rows edited after round 1** and nothing else, and its brief asked first
whether any of those edits had introduced a new error or landed only partially. It had. **Round 2 was
not clean, and rule 80 as scaled for this pass allows no third round** — what that means for the
delta is stated at the end of this section.

Twenty-two of the thirty-six rows were verified clean. The checker also re-proved several things from
the corpus rather than taking them on trust: that chapter 3733 is indeed the eleven agricultural-
labor-camp sections and R.C. 4781.01(W) reads as the corrected rows say; that the enrolled-act
arithmetic behind `edu-dv-protection-order-not-moved-oh` is exact (109 + 2 + 104 = 215 acts, zero
hits for "Sec. 3113.31", thirteen cross-references, two of them 136th G.A., no "former section
3113.31"); and that two zero-hit batteries reproduce.

**The two most serious.**

**A collapsed statutory exception, in the row whose whole subject it is.**
`edu-no-meter-conservation-charge-oh` said R.C. 5321.04(A)(6) requires water, hot water and heat "at
all times **unless the unit has its own direct public utility connection**". The section says:
'except where the building that includes the dwelling unit is not required by law to be equipped for
that purpose, **or** the dwelling unit is so constructed that heat or hot water is generated by an
installation within the exclusive control of the tenant **and** supplied by a direct public utility
connection'. Two alternatives, and the second needs all three of its elements. The row had dropped
the first alternative entirely and reduced the second to one element — so a landlord with
directly-metered units and a landlord-owned furnace would read it as saying a **non-waivable** supply
duty (R.C. 5321.13(A)) does not reach them. `edu-habitability-modifiable-oh`, in this same delta,
states the test correctly: "that second escape is structural and cumulative ... A submeter you read
and bill is not one." Two of the pass's own rows disagreed about a duty a landlord can be sued over.
The exception is now quoted verbatim with both alternatives and the exclusive-control element called
out.

**A hedge left standing after the row it hedged was resolved.** `edu-tenant-screening-oh` was saved
NEEDS_REVIEW on one missing fact — whether H.B. 50 was in force. The lead fetched the section's page
(Effective September 20, 2024; Latest Legislation House Bill 50 — 135th G.A.), upgraded the row to
VERIFIED, and wrote the resolved content into a new paragraph telling the landlord to **ask for the
certificate and record that they knew of it**. Paragraph 1 still said "Until our effective-date check
is finished ... **do not plan around the immunity**." R.C. 2953.26(F)(2) conditions the
negligent-leasing immunity on the decision-maker *having known of the certificate at the time of the
alleged negligence* — so the row discouraged, in one paragraph, the single step that buys the
immunity it recommended in the next, and a landlord reading cold could not tell which governed. Both
stale sentences are gone.

**Round 1's own failure mode recurred, and the checker was right to rank it third.** Ten rows in this
set carry round 1's "BOUND ADDED TO THE BODY" note; three confirmed-absence rows the lead edited in
the same pass were left with flat absence claims — `edu-no-fee-transparency-rule-oh`
("**no Ohio statute** makes charging a fee that the lease never mentions an unfair or deceptive trade
practice", against a search of 37 of Ohio's chapters), `edu-no-drug-free-addendum-rule-oh`, and
`edu-no-meter-conservation-charge-oh` again. The last is the one that costs money: its body said
"**Nothing caps** a markup, an administrative fee or a billing fee on a utility pass-through. Your
figure is yours", while its own notes said "R.C. ch. 4905, 4909 and 4933 and Ohio Adm.Code 4901:1 are
not held, **so this row does not claim Ohio utility regulation nowhere restricts a submetered water
surcharge**" — the four bodies of law most likely to restrict reselling utility service at a markup.
The body now names all four and says to read them before setting a figure. Three incidental absence
sentences in PRESENT rows got the same treatment (`edu-deposit-last-month-rent-oh`,
`edu-statutory-early-termination-oh`, `edu-unconscionability-oh`).

**The remaining eight, each fixed.**

- **`edu-dv-protection-order-not-moved-oh` characterised four unread sections.** The body described
R.C. 2919.26 as "a criminal temporary order" and R.C. 2151.34, 2903.213 and 2903.214 as covering
"people outside a family or household", while the row's own notes said the body "makes no claim about
their contents". The checker pulled the only cited source — S.B. 315's "Ohio protection order"
definition — from the corpus acts text and confirmed it names the numbers and says nothing about
criminal versus civil or household scope. This is the row a landlord uses to get a citation right,
and which order a tenant holds decides who may lawfully be on the premises, so the characterisations
are out; the numbers, their currency check, and an unread marker on all four remain.
- **`edu-dv-confidentiality-oh` left paragraph 1 unqualified** after the R.C. 111.99(D)
criminal-disclosure correction went in as a new paragraph — and paragraph 1 ends with a complete rule
of thumb, so a landlord could reasonably stop there and conclude there is no confidentiality duty,
when knowing re-disclosure of an address obtained under R.C. 111.43(E) is a first-degree
misdemeanour. The qualification is now inline, in the formulation the notes already carried: not "no
landlord duty" but "no duty unless you ask, and then a criminal one".
- **`edu-family-child-care-oh` overstated R.C. 3737.22.** The body had the fire marshal inspecting a
**licensed** type B home under (F) and (G). (F) only has the marshal "cooperate with" the director and
"recommend procedures for inspecting"; the section's one inspection duty is in (G) and reaches only an
**unlicensed** type B home, at the provider's own request, as a precondition of R.C. 3313.813
food-program approval. The row's notes had it right and the body contradicted them. Corrected, with
the R.C. 5104.052 question left open as unread — which puts the row's own advice (make consent
conditional on passing a fire inspection) back to work.
- **`edu-no-foreclosure-disclosure-oh`'s notes misdescribed the section they record reading.** They
had R.C. 5321.04 as "divisions (A)(1)-(9), (B) and (C)" and said "(A) still stops at (9)". It runs to
**(A)(10)** and ends at **(B)**; there is no division (C). The note also contradicted itself, since a
vetoed bill adding (A)(11) only makes sense against a section ending at (A)(10). The row's conclusion
is unaffected — (A)(10) is the SCRA limb, not a disclosure duty — but the notes are the record of what
was read, and as written they recorded a read that skipped the one limb importing a federal regime
into an Ohio landlord's statutory duties.
- **`edu-tenant-organizing-oh` carried a self-cancelling flag**: "has five grounds; the batch brief's
'five grounds' is also wrong". The brief's error was **four**. As written the flag said the correct
number was wrong, so nobody could act on it — and the R.C. 5321.03 ground count is this pass's known
error shape, corrected twice already. All five grounds are now named with their division numbers.
- **`edu-statutory-early-termination-oh` made R.C. 5321.02(B)'s remedies cumulative.** "the tenant may
terminate the rental agreement — **as well as** use your conduct as a defence ... or recover
possession". The statute lists them with "or"; only the damages-and-fees limb is additive ("In
addition"). The row states the identically structured R.C. 5321.07(B) correctly two sentences
earlier, and `edu-tenant-organizing-oh` states this very provision correctly, so the slip was
inconsistent within the row and across the delta. It overstated the tenant's settlement position
rather than misdirecting the drafting.
- **`edu-furnishings-included-oh` narrowed R.C. 3737.841(A)(4)** to a "**licensed**" type A family
child care home. The limb reads 'any child care center and any type A family child care home as
defined in section 5104.01 of the Revised Code', with no licensing qualifier, and R.C. 5104.01 was
not read in this pass — so a landlord whose tenant runs an unlicensed type A home would wrongly
conclude the R.C. 3737.842 flammability and label requirements do not reach their furniture, when
(C) prohibits *using* non-conforming seating furniture in a public occupancy. The word is out and the
open definitional question named where the sentence sits.
- **`edu-unconscionability-oh` asserted an identity its own notes disprove**: R.C. 4781.48 called a
"word-for-word twin" of R.C. 5321.14. Both of 5321.14's divisions read 'any clause **thereof**'; both
of 4781.48's read 'any clause **of it**'. Substantively the same, so the row's instruction (cite
5321.14 for an ordinary rental) was already right — but a row whose discipline is exact quotation
cannot claim word-for-word identity, because a drafter might then quote the wrong phrase as
R.C. 5321.14.

One finding was a judgment call rather than an error: `tenant-portal-oh`'s notes still described the
builder note as asserting no section number, when the round-1 fix deliberately made it name
R.C. 1306.04(C) and mark chapter 1306 unread. The substance was right; the audit trail was stale, and
is now current.

**A side note from outside the scope, acted on.** The checker noticed that `HELD_CHAPTERS.txt`'s
"already fetched" list had gone stale against `FETCHED_SECTIONS.md` — it omitted R.C. 3742.41/.42,
3749.01, 2711.01, 5104.054, 2953.26, 111.42/.43/.99 and others — so a later agent briefed off it would
wrongly conclude those rows cite unfetched sections. It checked the real file instead, so no finding
turned on it. That list is now **generated** from `FETCHED_SECTIONS.md`'s own headings, like the
held-chapter list above it, and marks which two entries are chapters rather than sections.

#### The (C)(7) correction, finished properly on the third pass

While fixing round 2's findings the lead found **four more rows** still asserting that
R.C. 5321.01(C)(7) is what puts manufactured-home-park units outside chapter 5321 —
`edu-no-extended-absence-notice-oh`, `edu-no-owner-move-in-rule-oh`,
`edu-no-term-change-notice-rule-oh` and `rules-regulations-oh` — plus the OH note segment added to
the tagged existing row `fees-and-charges-vi`. The first two correction passes had matched phrasings
built around "cited as the manufactured-home-park exclusion" and missed the verbs "keeps" and "puts".

So the fix was redone by method rather than by phrase: **every occurrence of the string
`5321.01(C)(7)` in the delta — bodies, notes and tag notes — was enumerated and classified by hand.**
There are **46**, and the final enumeration shows **none stale**: thirteen state the agricultural-
labour-camp position correctly, and the rest are the correction notes themselves. This is the third
correction pass on one error, which is the whole argument for the SOP proposal below that a
corrective edit must be swept exhaustively and re-verified rather than phrase-matched.

#### The cross-row consistency check, run because round 2 argued for it — and it found two more

Round 2's findings 1 and 5 were both one row contradicting another row in the same delta, and the
pass's sharpest error (R.C. 1923.01(B), above) was the same shape. So before delivering, the lead ran
the check that SOP proposal 8 below asks for: group every delta row by the statutory subdivisions it
cites in its **body**, and read the groups where two or more rows describe the same provision. It is
cheap — the citation is already in the text — and it is the only check in the pass that compares rows
to each other rather than to the statute.

It found **two more rows carrying round 2's finding 1**. Of the eleven delta rows that describe
R.C. 5321.04(A)(6), nine stated its exception correctly and three did not — the one round 2 caught,
plus `edu-no-submeter-interruption-regime-oh` and `edu-utility-transfer-cutoff-oh`, both of which said
the supply duty runs "at all times **unless the unit has its own direct public utility connection**".
Same collapse, same consequence: a non-waivable duty read as switched off by a direct meter alone.
Both are corrected to the statute's two alternatives, with the exclusive-control element named. A
re-run of the grouping shows the phrase gone from the delta.

The other high-count groups came back consistent, including two the pass had already had trouble with:
all **43** rows describing R.C. 5321.13(A) state it the same way and correctly, with division (F)'s
one-way exception; all **7** describing R.C. 5321.16(A) now carry "whichever is greater" or avoid the
threshold rather than misstate it; and all **8** describing R.C. 5321.04(B) correctly call it the
**tenant's** remedy — which is worth recording, because an error in the lead's own framing file had
called it the landlord's, and this confirms from the other direction that the error never reached a
row.

#### Rows edited after the last round, and what the two-round cap means

Rule 80 permits no third round, so the rows changed after round 2 have **not** been independently
checked. They are: `edu-no-meter-conservation-charge-oh`, `edu-tenant-screening-oh`,
`edu-family-child-care-oh`, `edu-tenant-organizing-oh`, `edu-statutory-early-termination-oh`,
`edu-no-foreclosure-disclosure-oh`, `edu-unconscionability-oh`, `edu-no-fee-transparency-rule-oh`,
`edu-no-drug-free-addendum-rule-oh`, `edu-deposit-last-month-rent-oh`,
`edu-dv-protection-order-not-moved-oh`, `edu-dv-confidentiality-oh`, `edu-furnishings-included-oh`,
`tenant-portal-oh`, `edu-limitation-of-actions-oh`, `edu-no-extended-absence-notice-oh`,
`edu-no-owner-move-in-rule-oh`, `edu-no-term-change-notice-rule-oh`, `rules-regulations-oh`,
`edu-no-submeter-interruption-regime-oh`, `edu-utility-transfer-cutoff-oh`, and the tag note on
`fees-and-charges-vi` — **twenty-two in all**.

Every one is a fix to a finding, written against statute text re-read for the purpose and quoted in
the row's notes, and the mechanical checks were re-run after each (the integrity suite, the
exhaustive `(C)(7)` enumeration, and a punctuation and orphaned-clause sweep over the whole delta,
all clean). But **two rounds of checking found errors in both rounds, and the second round's errors
were partly introduced by the first round's fixes** — which is the honest reason to say that a third
round would probably have found something rather than nothing. **Recommendation for Claude Code at
sync: re-read those twenty rows before merging them**, and treat the rule-80 cap as the reason this
pass cannot certify them rather than as evidence they are clean. The same point is raised as an SOP
proposal: the cap should bite on *rounds that find nothing*, not on rounds that keep finding things.


### §6 Findings on existing rows

**From the lead.** One conflict and three confirmations.

- **`hoa-compliance` — conflict.** Full analysis and the wording I would use are in §4 item 3. The row
is not changed; this goes to Claude Code at sync.
- **`keys` — no conflict.** Reasoning in §4 item 3.
- **`edu-bad-check-cure-windows-oh` — CONFIRMED CORRECT.** The B04 agent flagged it as unverifiable
because R.C. ch. 1319 is not in the corpus. I fetched both sections and the row's claim is exactly
right: R.C. 1319.16(A) gives the 'not more than thirty dollars or ten per cent of the face amount'
charge to a 'collection agency … designated to collect on a check … returned or dishonored', and
R.C. 1319.12(A)(2) excludes from 'collection agency' 'a person whose collection activities are
confined to and directly related to the operation of another business', the illustrative list
including '(c) Any retail seller collecting its own accounts'. The only thing worth adding at sync is
the two history lines: R.C. 1319.12 eff. 2018-03-23, H.B. 199, 132nd G.A.; R.C. 1319.16 eff.
2002-08-28, H.B. 464, 124th G.A.
- **`edu-landlord-utility-account-oh` — CONFIRMED CORRECT; a caution I raised is withdrawn.** Reading
R.C. 4933.121 section-open, I found a **five-day** tenant notice running only in the Nov 15 – Apr 15
window, against the row's '14-day landlord notice then 10-day per-unit tenant notice', and flagged it
for the utilities batch as a possible error. Having now fetched **Ohio Adm.Code 4901:1-18-08**
(eff. 2026-10-01), the row is right and I was wrong to doubt it: the 14-then-10 sequence is that
rule's division (A), which the row cites, and the rule says of it that 'This notice requirement shall
be complied with throughout the year.' The statute's five-day limb is a different and narrower winter
provision. The two do not conflict. **No change to the row, and nothing for Claude Code to fix here.**
- **`repair-escrow-exemption-notice-oh` and `edu-portfolio-thresholds-oh` — correct but incomplete**,
per the B02 agent's finding below: R.C. 5321.07(C) and (D) withdraw that *section* only, and neither
row says what survives it (R.C. 5321.12 damages, the R.C. 1923.061(B) counterclaim, R.C. 5321.04(B),
R.C. 5321.15(C), R.C. 1923.15). A landlord reading either row alone would reasonably conclude that
conditions claims are closed off.

- **`edu-bad-check-cure-windows-oh`, `edu-landlord-utility-account-oh`, `edu-statutory-forms-oh`,
`edu-alarm-duty-fire-code-oh`, `repair-escrow-exemption-notice-oh`, `tenant-maintenance`,
`edu-no-statutory-caps-oh`, `edu-habitability-escrow-oh`, `edu-termination-notice-periods-oh`,
`rent-payment`, `edu-no-abandoned-property-safe-harbor-oh` — all checked and none wrong.** Four of
those eleven were positively confirmed against statute text the canvass could not reach and the lead
fetched. `edu-mandatory-drug-termination-oh`'s own open flag ("1923.051 deadlines remain
chapter-level; verify section-open before stating day counts") is now **closed**: three working days
to serve, trial by the thirtieth calendar day, no continuances. But that row has a separate defect,
reported next.
- **`edu-mandatory-drug-termination-oh` states the trigger for Ohio's one mandatory eviction too
low — an error, found by the independent check at the end of this pass.** The row says: "once you
have actual knowledge of - or reasonable cause to believe - that the tenant, a household member, or
a guest has used, sold, or manufactured a controlled substance on the premises, you SHALL give the
three-day termination notice under 5321.17(C) and SHALL promptly bring an eviction action", and
later "Reasonable cause to believe is the trigger, and once you have it the statute says you SHALL
act." In Ohio that phrase is a term of art with a statutory definition, and the row uses it in its
ordinary sense. **R.C. 5321.05(C)(2)**, read section-open (eff. 1990-08-22, S.B. 258, 118th G.A.),
closes with: 'For purposes of this division, actual knowledge or reasonable cause to believe as
described in this division shall be determined in accordance with division (A)(6)(a)(i) of section
1923.02 of the Revised Code.' **R.C. 1923.02(A)(6)(a)(i)** then supplies the content: a landlord
has that state of mind 'if a search warrant was issued pursuant to Criminal Rule 41 or Chapter
2933.', the affidavit 'named or described the tenant', 'particularly described the tenant's premises
as the place to be searched' and 'named or described one or more controlled substances', 'the
warrant was properly executed by a law enforcement officer and any controlled substance described in
the affidavit was found by that officer during the search and seizure; and, subsequent to the search
and seizure, the landlord was informed by that or another law enforcement officer' of the violation.
So the mandatory track turns on a completed police chain, not on the landlord's own suspicion, and a
landlord who follows the row as written will serve a mandatory-sounding three-day termination in
cases where the statute imposes no duty at all — and may think a duty exists where the lease is the
only source of the obligation. **The fix is a trigger sentence, not a rewrite:** the row's
accelerated-schedule, R.C. 2925.13 and charge-not-required material is all correct and should stay.
One caution for whoever makes the edit: (A)(6)(a)(i) is phrased as a deeming provision ('has ... if')
rather than expressly as the only route, so whether anything short of the warrant chain can supply
the knowledge is unsettled and no case law was read. Both new Ohio rows on this subject in this
pass's delta — `drug-activity-prohibition-oh` and `edu-no-drug-free-addendum-rule-oh` — now state
both limbs and leave that ambiguity open rather than resolving it, which is the wording to match.

**Three incomplete-but-not-wrong rows, and a threshold worth adding.** Claude Code should look at
these at sync; the pass's fence barred editing them.

- **`repair-escrow-exemption-notice-oh`, and `edu-portfolio-thresholds-oh` for the same reason** —
R.C. 5321.07(C) and (D) withdraw that *section* only, and neither row says what survives it
(R.C. 5321.12 damages, the R.C. 1923.061(B) counterclaim, R.C. 5321.04(B), R.C. 5321.15(C),
R.C. 1923.15). A landlord reading either alone would reasonably conclude conditions claims are
closed off.
- **`edu-no-servicemember-lease-rule-oh`** — correct that Ohio gives no servicemember termination
right, but **R.C. 5321.04(A)(10) does make SCRA compliance an Ohio landlord duty**, and it cites the
Act by the stale "117 Stat. 2835, 50 U.S.C. App. 501". A sentence about (A)(10) may belong there.
- **`edu-portfolio-thresholds-oh` also deserves a sixth threshold:** R.C. 3749.01(F)'s "no more than
three families" line, which decides whether a pool is a private residential pool outside
R.C. 3749.01 to 3749.10 or a public swimming pool licensed by the board of health. It sits alongside
the three-or-fewer-units escrow exemption and R.C. 3781.06(C)(9)'s definition of a residential
building.

- **`edu-portfolio-thresholds-oh` — its standing caveat is now discharged, and one sentence
overstates.** The B07 agent read R.C. 4112.02 whole plus 4112.08, 4112.16 and 4112.99, which answers
the row's own note that the rest of chapter 4112 had not been read. The row is right that no size
test exempts anyone from the core housing prohibitions — but its sentence "Fair housing under Chapter
4112 has NO small landlord exemption at all" is written at chapter level, and the chapter has **two**
size tests: **R.C. 4112.01(A)(2)** defines "employer" as four or more persons, so a landlord with
three employees sits outside division (A) altogether, and **R.C. 4112.02(H)(22)** imposes the
design-and-construct duty only on "covered multifamily dwellings" — four or more units, first
occupancy on or after 30 June 1992. Both count four, like three of the row's existing five
thresholds. It also deserves R.C. 3749.01(F)'s "no more than three families" pool line as a sixth.
- **An eviction row should say how to serve a tenant in the address confidentiality programme, and
none does.** R.C. 111.43(D)(4)(a) makes service on the Secretary of State service on the participant
under Civ.R. 4.2 — but a **R.C. 1923.04(A) notice to leave is not service of process**, so the two do
not obviously connect, and a landlord whose tenant's only address of record is the Secretary's has no
guidance anywhere in the library. Raised by the B07 agent; it needs its own look, and changing an
existing eviction row was outside this pass's fence.
- **`edu-retaliation-oh`'s own flag can be cleared.** Its notes say R.C. 5321.03 was "NOT read
section-open in this pass, flagged". The B09 agent read it section-open (eff. 2023-10-03, H.B. 33,
135th G.A.) and it bears the row out, including the (B)(3) termination remedy the row already states.
- **`holdover-oh` is structurally unlike every other state's, and the shape question should be
decided once.** Ohio prices its holdover **inside** the base clause using `{{holdover_daily_rate}}`,
where the Virgin Islands and American Samoa keep a bare `Holdover` clause plus a separate optional
rate clause. Not restructured — the fence bars changing an existing row, and the new education row
works either way — but worth settling across the library rather than state by state.
- **`edu-no-abandoned-property-safe-harbor-oh` is now confirmed twice, from different directions**:
the eviction batch from the chapter structure, the termination batch from the writ text itself
(R.C. 1923.13(A) contains no notice, valuation or disposal machinery; all of it is in (B) with
R.C. 1923.12 and 1923.14, which run only against a manufactured-home-park resident or the resident's
estate, and "abandon" appears nowhere in chapter 5321). Two agents that never saw each other's work.
- **A term threshold for `edu-portfolio-thresholds-oh`'s neighbourhood, though not for that row.**
Ohio has three lease-term thresholds and none of them is in chapter 5321: **one year**
(R.C. 1335.05 bars the *action* absent a signed writing), **three years** (R.C. 5301.08 — above it,
R.C. 5301.01(A) requires the lessor's signature to be acknowledged before a notary or other named
official, and the lease should be recorded), and **no ceiling** on the term itself.
- **`edu-flag-display-oh` is incomplete, and two agents found it independently.** It carries
R.C. 5321.131(A) and (B) but nothing from **(D)** — "Any provision of a rental agreement that
violates this section is an unconscionable term under section 5321.14" — which is **Ohio's only
statutory per-se unconscionable lease clause** — and nothing from **(C)**, which relieves a landlord
who received no (B) notification from "any damages, fines, or costs" arising from the tenant's flag
pole or bracket. (C) is a protection in the landlord's favour that the row omits, and so is **(E)**: "Nothing in division (B) of this section exempts a tenant from a provision in a lease agreement that requires a tenant, at the termination of a lease, to return the premises in the same condition as they were in when the tenant took possession." So the section carries **three** landlord-favourable limbs the row does not have - (C), (D) and (E) - and (E) is the one a landlord most needs, because it is what lets a restoration obligation survive a flag-pole or bracket installation the landlord could not refuse.
- **`edu-self-help-eviction-ban-oh` may understate R.C. 5321.15(A).** Read whole, (A) reaches not only
utilities and locks but "exclusion from the premises, or threat of any unlawful act", and the
protection expressly extends to "a tenant whose right to possession has terminated" — which is the
key wording for the trespass and former-tenant questions. Worth checking the body against the section.
- **`edu-no-ev-charging-right-oh` may rest on unread chapters.** It sits on the same ground as
`portable-solar`: R.C. ch. 4928 and the relevant parts of ch. 5301 are not held. The pass is **not**
asserting the row is wrong — but if it states a confirmed absence rather than a bounded one, its
bound may need the same treatment the new `edu-no-solar-rights-oh` was given.
- **Two internal tensions in the fair-housing material, recorded and not resolved.**
**R.C. 4112.02(H)(17)** permits five applicant inquiries, two of which **Ohio Adm.Code 4112-5-07(B)**
does not except: the statute prevails over the rule, but the rule is the safe course, and the rows
say so. And **Ohio Adm.Code 4112-5-04(B)** cites "division (H)(7)" for the record-keeping safe
harbour where the prohibition now sits at **(H)(8)** — a stale cross-reference in the rule itself.

**Two rule-26 checks for Claude Code before anything is tagged at sync.**
`nonrefundable-fees-wv` (shared, VI/AS/MP) — **do not add OH**: R.C. 5321.01(E) with R.C. 5321.13(A)
make such a term unrecognised in Ohio. `detector-care-la` — its id indicates a Louisiana basis, so
Ohio got its own clause rather than a tag; check its `lease_clause_basis` before OH is ever added to
its states list.

**A second, separate possible defect in `hoa-compliance`.** R.C. 5311.19(B)(1) lets the association
sue only "as the unit owner's agent, in the name of the unit owner". If that clause — or any Ohio
clause — lets an association act **in its own name** against a tenant, that is distinct from the
R.C. 5321.13(D) indemnity defect reported above and needs its own correction. Not re-read, because
the fence bars re-auditing an existing row; flagged so both can be looked at together.

- (`abandonment-and-mitigation`) edu-no-abandoned-property-safe-harbor-oh is CONFIRMED CORRECT and confirmed park-only a second time: I read R.C. 1923.13 section-open and (A), the ordinary writ form, contains no notice, valuation or disposal machinery at all - every bit of it is in (B) and in R.C. 1923.12 and 1923.14, which run only against a manufactured home park resident or the resident's estate. I also confirmed 'abandon' appears nowhere in chapter 5321. Division of subject: that row is about the BELONGINGS; this row is about the MONEY and the tenancy (does the lease end, is there acceleration, must you re-rent). edu-casualty-and-mitigation-waivable-oh carries the case-law mitigation duty and its waivability; I did not restate it and did not touch it.
- (`alarm-tampering-fee`) The batch brief lists `detector-care-la` (LEASE_CLAUSE, states VI,AS,MP) as the example clause for this topic. I did not read it, but its id indicates a Louisiana-derived basis, and per rule 26 a shared row whose lease_clause_basis or notes rest on another state's statute gets Ohio its own row even if the wording fits - which is why I wrote `alarm-tampering-fee-oh` rather than tagging. Worth a check of `detector-care-la`'s basis before adding OH to its states list. Separately, Ohio's existing `edu-alarm-duty-fire-code-oh` (topic_key `alarm-duties`) correctly states that Ohio's alarm duty comes from code except in a high-rise; nothing I read contradicts it.
- (`animal-care-access`) TAG CONSIDERED AND DECLINED on `animal-care-access-vi` (states VI;AS;MP;CO - does not carry OH). Its fallback names 'an animal warden, a peace officer, or the Humane Society or an animal shelter' and its notes rest on 14 V.I.C. 1747(2) and 14 V.I.C. 187(a) - Virgin Islands provisions with no Ohio counterpart I could read, since ch. 959 and ch. 1717 are not held (rule 26). The Ohio clause also has to do something the VI clause does not: characterise the care as an 'agreed service', which is the only limb of R.C. 5321.05(B) a lease can fill. Consistency check passed: `pet-policy` and `pet-insurance-requirement` (both shared, both carry OH) say nothing about entry or emergency care, so there is no overlap.
- (`association-obligations-disclosure`) No new finding. The shared clause `hoa-compliance` is the lease-side row for this family and the lead has already reported its conflict with R.C. 5321.13(D) separately; per the batch brief I did not re-open it and wrote nothing that contradicts the finding that an association fine is the owner's liability. Note for the lead: R.C. 5311.19(B)(1) lets the association bring the eviction 'as the unit owner's agent, in the name of the unit owner' - if `hoa-compliance` or any Ohio clause purports to let the association act in its own name against the tenant, that is a second, separate problem with it.
- (`carpet-cleaning`) carpet-cleaning-ar (states AR;MS;PR;VI;AS;MP;KS;NE) is conditioned on the carpets having been professionally cleaned immediately before the tenancy began - that condition is Alaska's statutory one (AS 34.03.120(c)), not Ohio's. Ohio has no such condition, so the shared clause should NOT simply be extended to OH; Ohio gets its own clause instead (rule 26). The shared clause is not wrong for the states it carries.
- (`cold-weather-vacate-notice`) Divides from extended-absence-notice (same batch), which carries the general absence-notice point and the lease term itself; this row is the cold-weather and heat-duty limb. Also divides from edu-landlord-duties-oh, which carries R.C. 5321.04 generally - the (A)(6) exception clauses and the no-shifting consequence are the new content here.
- (`collection-fee`) edu-bad-check-cure-windows-oh - CONFIRMED CORRECT, no change needed. The B04 agent flagged this row as unverifiable because R.C. ch. 1319 is not in the corpus. The lead fetched both sections section-open on 2026-10-30 and the row's claim is exactly right: R.C. 1319.16(A) gives the 'not more than thirty dollars or ten per cent of the face amount' charge to a 'collection agency has been designated to collect on a check ... returned or dishonored', and R.C. 1319.12(A)(2) excludes from 'collection agency' 'a person whose collection activities are confined to and directly related to the operation of another business', the illustrative list including '(c) Any retail seller collecting its own accounts'. The row's sentence - 'the $30-or-10% figure you will find quoted in Ohio lease templates is R.C. 1319.16, which caps what a COLLECTION AGENCY may charge, and 1319.12(A)(2) excludes a landlord collecting its own account' - stands as written. Recorded as a positive confirmation, not an error. The one thing the row's notes could add at sync is the two Effective / Latest Legislation lines: R.C. 1319.12 eff. 2018-03-23, H.B. 199, 132nd G.A.; R.C. 1319.16 eff. 2002-08-28, H.B. 464, 124th G.A.
- (`condition-inspection`) TAG CONSIDERED AND DECLINED on `move-in-condition-report-as` (states AS;MP;WY - does not carry OH). It is close, but it covers only the move-in report and an offered move-out inspection, and it says nothing about the itemised written notice R.C. 5321.16(B) requires within thirty days, which is where an Ohio landlord actually loses the money. Its WY segment also rests on Wyo. Stat. 1-21-1204(a)(viii) and 1-21-1208(a) making the beginning condition the statutory yardstick, which Ohio has no counterpart to (rule 26). CONFIRMATION, not a defect: `edu-no-deposit-standards-disclosure-oh` and `edu-no-deposit-cost-schedule-oh` both state the no-condition-report / no-cost-schedule position, and both read correctly against R.C. 5321.16(B) as I read it section-open; the quoted limb 'damages that the landlord has suffered by reason of the tenant's noncompliance with section 5321.05 of the Revised Code or the rental agreement' is accurate in `edu-no-deposit-cost-schedule-oh`.
- (`confession-of-judgment`) ALREADY ANSWERED by `edu-exculpation-indemnity-ban-oh` (topic_key prohibited-lease-terms, LANDLORD_EDUCATION, PROHIBITED, 'Two Ohio Lease Terms That Are Never Recognised'). Its bodyText carries R.C. 5321.13(B) in terms - 'no warrant of attorney to confess judgment is recognised in any rental agreement, or in any other landlord-tenant agreement for the recovery of rent or damages. A cognovit provision in an Ohio residential lease is dead on arrival.' - and its notes record it as READ SECTION-OPEN 2026-09-27 with the correct history line (effective 1990-08-22, S.B. 258, 118th G.A.). That row is CORRECT and complete for this topic; no competing row written. Corroborated this session: the corpus's only two 'warrant of attorney' sections are R.C. 5321.13 and R.C. 4781.47 (manufactured-home park rental agreement terms, units that R.C. 5321.01(C)(7) excludes from chapter 5321 anyway), so R.C. 5321.13(B) is the whole of the residential rule inside the corpus. CONTEXT NOT NEEDED BUT WORTH THE LEAD'S NOTE: Ohio's general cognovit statute is R.C. 2323.13, and chapter 2323 is confirmed NOT held (HELD_CHAPTERS.txt) and could not be fetched. It is not required to answer this topic, because R.C. 5321.13(B) is categorical for residential leases and reaches agreements outside the lease as well; R.C. 2323.13 would only add the general warning-language and consumer-transaction limits. The batch brief's court-rules question (confessed ejectment and the supersedeas amount on appeal) was NOT researched - Ohio court rules and forms are outside the corpus (supremecourt.ohio.gov) and R.C. 2505 is confirmed not held.

One omission worth the lead's note: it describes the tenant's habitability route without the R.C. 5321.07(C) three-or-fewer-units exemption or the (D) student-tenant exemption, so a landlord reading it would assume the escrow route always exists. Ohio already has `repair-escrow-exemption-notice-oh` (LEASE_CLAUSE, CONDITIONAL, OH) for giving the (C) notice and `edu-habitability-escrow-oh` for the mechanism, so the exemptions are covered elsewhere in the set - but not in that row, and this row now states them in the disaster context. Not an error; not edited.

Its body says "Ohio has no statewide domestic violence lease-termination right, no DV anti-termination protection, and no DV lock-change duty" - my independent search confirms all three limbs: ch. 5321's only 'domestic violence' text is the two scope exclusions at R.C. 5321.01(C)(9)(b)(ii) and (C)(10), and R.C. 3113.31 contains no termination right and no landlord word. Note the overlap: that row already asserts the no-lock-change point in passing, so edu-no-dv-lockchange-oh is the row that carries the subject (rule 57) and should cross-reference rather than contradict it. - (`emergency-contact`) DO NOT EXTEND `tenant-death-contact-nh` TO OHIO. That shared clause (states NH;ME;VT;AK;AR;MS;GU;VI;AS;MP) is the library's existing contact-designation clause and its wording is otherwise Ohio-compatible, but it ends by promising that the landlord 'will handle any property left at the property under this Lease's Handling of Property Left Behind Section and applicable law'. OHIO HAS NO SUCH SECTION AND NO SUCH LAW: `edu-no-abandoned-property-safe-harbor-oh` (abandoned-property, PROHIBITED, OH) is titled 'Ohio Gives You No Way to Deal With Left-Behind Belongings', and R.C. 5321.15(B) makes seizing a tenant's possessions for rent without a court order actionable for all damages plus reasonable attorney's fees. Tagging the NH clause would therefore point an Ohio landlord at a procedure that does not exist and at the one act Ohio penalises. Its per-state notes also all rest on 'no statute addresses a tenant's death' in those jurisdictions plus each one's own abandoned-property section (N.H. Rev. Stat. Ann. 540-A:3, VII; AS 34.03.260; and so on), which is exactly the rule 26 situation. Hence an Ohio clause, deliberately silent on left-behind property. SEPARATELY: `edu-tenant-death-oh` is correct as written on this point and this row does not contradict it.
- (`employee-screening`) edu-portfolio-thresholds-oh - ITS OWN STANDING CAVEAT IS NOW PARTLY DISCHARGED, AND ITS CHAPTER-4112 SENTENCE IS AN OVERSTATEMENT. I did not change the row. Its notes record: '4112.01 and 4112.024 were read in full, the rest of ch. 4112 was not, so an exemption elsewhere in the chapter has not been ruled out.' I have now read R.C. 4112.02 whole (all 22 limbs of division (H)) plus 4112.08, 4112.16 and 4112.99, and searched 'exempt' and 'shall not apply' across the chapter. Result: the row is CORRECT that no size test exempts anyone from the core housing prohibitions - division (H) binds 'any person', and R.C. 4112.01(A)(1) sweeps in any owner, lessor, manager, agent or employee. But the row's sentence 'Fair housing under Chapter 4112 has NO small landlord exemption at all - every landlord is covered regardless of size' is written at chapter level, and there are TWO size tests inside ch. 4112: (1) R.C. 4112.01(A)(2) defines 'employer' as 'a person employing four or more persons within the state', so a landlord with three or fewer employees is outside division (A) altogether - a sixth threshold, on the employment limb; and (2) R.C. 4112.02(H)(22), which is inside division (H), imposes its design-and-construct accessibility duty only on 'covered multifamily dwellings', defined in the same limb as 'buildings consisting of four or more units if such buildings have one or more elevators and ground floor units in other buildings consisting of four or more units', and only for first occupancy on or after 1992-06-30 - a seventh threshold, on the housing limb. Both count four, like three of the row's existing five, which is exactly the trap the row exists to warn about. Suggested wording for whoever updates it: narrow the sentence to the chapter's core housing prohibitions rather than the chapter.
- (`environmental-event-termination`) Ohio's casualty position is already carried and is correct: casualty-landlord-termination-oh (LEASE_CLAUSE, topic casualty-termination, SERVES_LANDLORD) quotes R.C. 5301.11 verbatim in its notes and offers the optional landlord termination right; edu-casualty-and-mitigation-waivable-oh carries the waivability point. I read both in full from lease-clauses.csv before drafting and divided from them: this row is about environmental events that leave the building standing (flood damage, contamination, condemnation, a declared disaster) and about the R.C. 1923.15 re-rental bar, neither of which either existing row mentions. Nothing in either row is wrong.

That is a weaker instrument than Arizona's browser-run official search - recorded honestly per instruction 15. Terms run: electric vehicle; electric vehicle charging; charging station.' (3) So the B11 agent's specific worry - that the row 'may rest on R.C. ch. 4928 and parts of ch. 5301 that are NOT held' - is misdirected. The row did not rest on the 37-chapter corpus at all; it rested on a full-text search of the WHOLE Revised Code, which is a broader instrument than any battery in this pass can run, and it names R.C. 4928.01 as a hit it opened and did not rely on. Its substantive conclusion therefore stands and needs no correction. (4) What the row does share with the pre-correction solar row is placement: the bound lives only in the notes, while the body states the absence flat. NOT EDITED, per the fence. Recommended action: a notes-only or body-only bound insertion by the lead, not a re-verification."

- (`exculpatory-clauses`) ALREADY ANSWERED by `edu-exculpation-indemnity-ban-oh` (topic_key prohibited-lease-terms, LANDLORD_EDUCATION, PROHIBITED). Its bodyText carries R.C. 5321.13(D) in full effect - no tenant agreement to the exculpation or limitation of ANY liability of the landlord arising under law, and no indemnity - and it already names the whole clause family ('Landlord shall not be liable for', 'Tenant releases Landlord from', 'Tenant agrees to indemnify, defend and hold Landlord harmless', and a hold-harmless buried in a pet, parking, storage or utilities clause), says mutuality, narrowing and 'to the extent permitted by law' do not help, records that Ohio has NO common-areas carve-out unlike K.S.A. 58-2547(a)(4), and tells the landlord to use insurance instead of drafting. Its notes are READ SECTION-OPEN 2026-09-27 with the right history line (effective 1990-08-22, S.B. 258, 118th G.A.). That row is CORRECT and complete; no competing row written. Corroborated this session: 'exculpation' returns exactly two sections in the corpus, R.C. 5321.13 and R.C. 4781.47 (manufactured-home parks, excluded from chapter 5321 by R.C. 5321.01(C)(7)), and 'exculpatory' and 'release of liability' return 0 - so R.C. 5321.13(D) is the whole residential rule inside the corpus and the existing row misses nothing. The two open points that row already flags remain open and were not researched here: whether an Ohio court has severed a void exculpation clause or voided a whole lease, and whether (D) reaches a waiver of subrogation in a tenant's insurance clause. R.C. 5321.14 remains the separate unconscionability ceiling.

Not an error - a satisfied caution. Worth recording in that row's notes at the next edit pass.

Its LA segment rests on La. Civ. Code arts. 2683, 2687 and 2688, but the clause text is generic and other states have been tagged onto it with their own segments. The clause takes no separate deposit, so any security for the furniture stays inside R.C. 5321.01(E) (money OR property) and R.C. 5321.16; the return-in-same-condition and prompt-notice terms are permitted terms under R.C. 5321.06 and sit alongside the tenant's R.C. 5321.05(A)(6) duty not to destroy, deface, damage or remove any fixture, appliance or other part of the premises; the clause creates no lien, so R.C. 5321.15(B) is not engaged. R.C. 5321.14 is the only ceiling.' I have not edited the row.

- (`guarantor-renewal`) {
 "id": "FETCHED_SECTIONS.md (shared file, not a row) - R.C. 1335.05",
 "finding": "A SHARED FILE IS INCOMPLETE IN A WAY THAT BLOCKED THIS TOPIC, and the briefing asked me to flag rather than reconcile. FETCHED_SECTIONS.md quotes R.C. 1335.05 as: \"No action shall be brought whereby to charge the defendant ... upon a contract or sale of lands, tenements, or hereditaments, or interest in or concerning them, or upon an agreement that is not to be performed within one year from the making thereof; unless ...\". The ellipsis after 'the defendant' is where the suretyship limb of Ohio's statute of frauds sits - the limb about a special promise to answer for the debt, default or miscarriage of another person, which is precisely the limb a lease guaranty turns on and the Ohio counterpart to the Kentucky statute (KRS 371.065) this topic names. I did not reconstruct it from memory and did not state its terms. The file's own 'How to use these two' note frames both sections as 'the Ohio statute of frauds for leases', which is true but is not the whole of R.C. 1335.05 and reads as if it were. REQUEST: refetch R.C. 1335.05 whole and record the elided limbs, then this topic can move from NEEDS_REVIEW to VERIFIED. Nothing in any existing row depends on the gap as far as I could see - no row in oh_existing_rows.md or pass_rows_so_far.md has a guaranty topic for Ohio."
}

The rows that DO describe an association acting directly against a tenant are education rows for other states only and must never be extended to OH: 'edu-hoa-rental-rules-ut' ('If an owner falls behind on assessments, the association may demand that the tenant pay rent to the association instead'), 'edu-condominium-leasing-mi' ('it may sue to evict the tenant and for damages' / 'a tenant who refuses may be served a notice to quit by the association'), 'edu-association-leasing-rules-fl' (rent demands), and 'edu-hoa-wa' ('an association may enforce its rules against tenants and impose fines'). Each is correct for its own state and each is wrong for Ohio on both limbs - the agency limb and the absence of any Ohio rent-redirection provision. Recorded as a cross-state contamination risk, not as an error in those rows. SEPARATELY, on the already-settled 'hoa-compliance' conflict: its second sentence is also unsafe for Ohio in a way the pass has not stated, and it is worth one line in the log - the sentence says the fine 'will be Tenant's responsibility', which is the indemnity form R.C. 5321.13(D) refuses to recognise, whereas the Oregon override 'hoa-compliance-or' already uses the safe form ('Tenant will reimburse Landlord, as damages for that breach, for any fine or assessment the association imposes on Landlord because of it'). That is the model an Ohio override would follow. NOT EDITED, per the fence."

- (`holdover-rate`) IMPORTANT STRUCTURAL FINDING. Ohio already prices its holdover inside the base clause: holdover-oh (LEASE_CLAUSE, topic_key holdover, CONSTRAINED_TERM) carries 'Tenant will pay holdover rent at the daily rate of {{holdover_daily_rate}}, which the parties agree is a reasonable estimate of Landlord's loss and not a penalty.' In VI and several other states the library instead keeps a bare 'Holdover' clause and offers the stipulated daily charge as a SEPARATE optional holdover-rate clause (holdover-rate-vi). So writing an Ohio holdover-rate CLAUSE would duplicate holdover-oh and break one-subject-per-row; I have written the education row the landlord needs in order to fill {{holdover_daily_rate}} instead, which holdover-oh's clause body cannot carry because clause bodies take no citations. holdover-oh's notes are correct and I did not change them. Taylor may want to decide whether Ohio should follow the VI split (bare holdover clause + optional rate clause) for consistency - see question_for_taylor.

This row cites R.C. 4112.02(H)(19) only as a constraint on how you answer a request, not as a termination right.
- (`inspection-notice-penalty`) CORRECTION TO THE BATCH BRIEF, not to a row: the brief states 'R.C. 5321.04(B) gives the landlord an injunction and damages where a tenant unreasonably withholds'. Read section-open, R.C. 5321.04(B) runs the other way - it is the TENANT'S remedy against the landlord for a bad entry. The landlord's injunction 'to compel access under division (B) of this section', with actual damages and reasonable attorney's fees, is in R.C. 5321.05(C)(1). The corpus wins. No existing Ohio row repeats the error: the OH note segment on the shared `landlords-access` row correctly reads '(B)' as the tenant's remedies, and nothing in `oh_existing_rows.md` carries the entry-remedy subject, so this row is the first.
- (`key-control-policy`) Ohio already carries the shared clause row `keys` (topic_key `keys`, LEASE_CLAUSE, RECOMMENDED, 'Keys'). I did not read its bodyText, so I cannot say whether it already covers return-of-keys at move-out and a bar on tenant-added locks. If it does, no further Ohio clause is needed for this subject; if it rests on another state's statute, rule 26 would call for an Ohio clause. Flagging rather than acting.
- (`landlord-breach-remedy`) repair-escrow-exemption-notice-oh (LEASE_CLAUSE, OH, basis REQUIRED_DISCLOSURE: R.C. 5321.07(C)) is CORRECT AS WRITTEN - its body says only 'Tenant's remedies under Ohio Revised Code section 5321.07 do not apply to Landlord', which tracks the statute's own 'This section does not apply'. This is a gap, not an error: neither the clause's body nor its notes say what the exemption leaves untouched, and a landlord reading it alone would reasonably believe conditions claims are closed off. R.C. 5321.07(C) and (D) are section-limited, so R.C. 5321.12 damages, the R.C. 1923.061(B) counterclaim, R.C. 5321.04(B) entry remedies, R.C. 5321.15(C) self-help damages and the R.C. 1923.15 inspection and no-re-rental order all still run. I did not edit the row. Suggested fix, for your decision: add one sentence to that clause's notes pointing at edu-landlord-breach-remedy-oh. The same caution applies to edu-portfolio-thresholds-oh, which presents 5321.07(C) as one of five portfolio tests without saying how little it buys.
- (`landlord-remedies-termination`) Divides from edu-tenant-cure-period-oh (which cure track to use), edu-three-day-notice-language-oh (the notice wording), edu-self-help-eviction-ban-oh (the self-help ban), edu-for-cause-eviction-oh (the grounds) and edu-waiver-by-acceptance-oh. The new content here is the remedy architecture: R.C. 1923.03's no-bar rule, R.C. 1923.081 joinder and its good-cause continuance, R.C. 1923.061(B)'s counterclaim set-off, and the forum rules in R.C. 1901.17, 1901.18(A)(8) and 1901.181(A)(1). None of those sections appears in any existing Ohio row.
- (`law-enforcement-cooperation`) ROW IDS IN THE BATCH BRIEF DO NOT MATCH WHAT IS SAVED. - (`nonresident-owner-agent`) {
 "id": "landlord-identity-oh",
 "finding": "Not an error - a caveat that can now be closed. That row's notes say: \"Entity-specific rules about principal place of business in the county were read at chapter level; verify section-open before relying on them in the builder's field validation.\" I have now read R.C. 5321.18 SECTION-OPEN from the corpus chapter page (eff. 1974-11-04, Latest Legislation S.B. 103, 110th G.A.) and division (A) reads exactly as the row assumes: 'If the owner or the owner's agent is a corporation, partnership, limited partnership, association, trust, or other entity, the address shall be the principal place of business in the county in which the residential property is situated or if there is no place of business in such county then its principal place of business in this state, and shall include the name of the person in charge thereof.' The builder's field validation can rely on it. Worth noting for the builder: an out-of-state entity owner with no Ohio place of business cannot satisfy the limb literally, and R.C. 5321.18(C)'s sanction is waiver of the landlord's right to the tenant's R.C. 5321.07 and 5321.08 notices."
}
- (`owner-move-in-reservation`) owner-occupancy-termination-ar (LEASE_CLAUSE, SERVES_LANDLORD, AR;MS;PR;GU;VI;AS;MP;WY;KS;NE) reads correctly for Ohio and is tagged rather than duplicated - its basis is SERVES_LANDLORD and the text names no Arkansas law, so rule 26 does not force an Ohio clause. Following the VI and AS pattern for this topic, this file carries both the tag and an Ohio education row. SEPARATE FINDING WORTH ACTING ON: edu-retaliation-oh's notes end 'R.C. 5321.03 lets a landlord still bring a possession action on the grounds listed there notwithstanding 5321.02 - NOT read section-open in this pass, flagged.' I read R.C. 5321.03 section-open on 2026-10-30 (eff. 2023-10-03, H.B. 33, 135th G.A.) and it bears that row out; the four grounds are default in rent, a tenant-caused violation, compliance requiring alteration/remodeling/demolition that would effectively deprive the tenant of the unit, and holding over, plus (A)(5) the registered-offender ground. That flag can be cleared.
- (`part5-nonwaivable`) ALREADY ANSWERED by `edu-one-way-delegation-oh` (topic_key tenant-repair-agreement, LANDLORD_EDUCATION, CONSTRAINED, 'Ohio Duty-Shifting Runs One Direction Only'). Its bodyText states the nonwaiver rule exactly: 'R.C. 5321.13(A) makes the whole of Chapter 5321 non-waivable except through division (F), and (F) permits only one move: the landlord assuming the tenant's 5321.05 duties. There is no route in the other direction... R.C. 5321.13(E) closes the obvious workaround by barring any agreement that lets you receive rent free of the obligation to comply with 5321.04.' That is the whole of Ohio's Part-V-equivalent nonwaivability rule and the row is CORRECT; no competing row written. VERIFIED AGAINST THE PRIMARY TEXT THIS SESSION: R.C. 5321.13 READ SECTION-OPEN (effective 1990-08-22, Latest Legislation S.B. 258, 118th G.A.); (A) verbatim is 'No provision of this chapter may be modified or waived by any oral or written agreement except as provided in division (F) of this section', and (F) verbatim excludes only the R.C. 5321.05(A)(9) drug-activity duty from what the landlord may assume. The corpus text matches OHIO_FRAMING.md item 2 word for word in all six divisions. TWO BOUNDS WORTH THE LEAD'S NOTE, both already consistent with the existing row rather than corrections to it. First, (A)'s reach is 'any provision of this chapter' - so a tenant right that lives OUTSIDE chapter 5321 is not protected by it, which is precisely the point `edu-casualty-and-mitigation-waivable-oh` carries for R.C. 5301.11 and the common-law duty to re-rent. Second, Ohio has a separate and wider nonwaiver rule for execution exemptions in R.C. 2329.661(B) ('every promise, agreement, or contract insofar as it seeks to waive the exemption laws of this state is void'), which is not a chapter 5321 provision and is now carried by the new `edu-homestead-waiver-oh`. The only other corpus section using 'modified or waived' is R.C. 4781.47 (manufactured-home park rental agreement terms), and R.C. 5321.01(C)(7) puts those units outside chapter 5321. R.C. 5321.18(C)'s 'waived' is a different thing - the landlord's own waiver of the 5321.07(A) and 5321.08(A) notices as the penalty for not giving the owner-and-agent notice.
- (`periodic-services-entry`) TAG CONSIDERED AND DECLINED on the shared clause `scheduled-services-entry-la` (states LA;AR;MS;VI;AS;MP;CO;KS - does not carry OH). Its per-state notes all rest on the premise that the state sets NO entry notice ('Louisiana has no entry statute', 'Arkansas sets no statutory entry notice or hours', 'no Mississippi entry statute'), which is the opposite of Ohio's position, so tagging it would attach an Ohio segment to a basis Ohio does not share (rule 26). Its body also omits the 'agreed services / agreed repairs' characterisation that is what does the work under R.C. 5321.05(B) in Ohio, and omits the R.C. 5321.04(A)(8) emergency/impracticable carve-out. Hence an Ohio row. NOT A DEFECT, FOR THE RECORD: the shared `landlords-access` row (Access & Entry, SERVES_LANDLORD, carries OH) already states the general 24-hour entry notice and its OH note segment is correct, including 'Remedies at (B): actual damages, injunction, statutory attorney's fees, or termination'; this row cross-refers to it rather than repeating it.
- (`political-access`) GAP CONFIRMED IN `edu-flag-display-oh` (tenant-display-rights, LANDLORD_EDUCATION, OH). Its body states R.C. 5321.131's prohibition and the (B) notice-and-consultation duty accurately, and its notes record the (B) scope correction - but it carries NOTHING from division (D), which is the consequence: 'Any violation of this section is against public policy and unenforceable. Any provision of a rental agreement that violates this section is an unconscionable term under section 5321.14 of the Revised Code.' That is the only statutory per-se unconscionable lease term in Ohio, and a landlord reading the existing row would not learn that a no-flags clause is not merely unenforceable but is deemed unconscionable, with R.C. 5321.14's power to refuse to enforce the rental agreement as a whole. Also absent from it: division (C), which relieves a landlord who received no (B) notification of 'any damages, fines, or costs associated with any issues arising from the placement of the flag pole or the bracket by the tenant' - a protection in the landlord's favour. NOT AN ERROR, an incompleteness; per the briefing I have not edited it. This row and `religious-cultural-display` both carry (D) as the contrast, but the lead may want (C) and (D) added to the flag row itself.
- (`portable-solar`) edu-no-ev-charging-right-oh (topic ev-charging) states that Ohio gives a tenant no right to install EV charging. That conclusion rests on the same two unread chapters as this topic (R.C. ch. 4928 and ch. 5301). I have NOT read that row's full bodyText or notes and am not asserting it is wrong - but if it claims a confirmed absence rather than a located gap, its bound may need the same treatment as this row once ch. 4928 and ch. 5301 are fetched. Worth the lead's eye in the same pass.
- (`post-eviction-property`) `edu-no-abandoned-property-safe-harbor-oh` is confirmed correct on a fresh whole-chapter read of ch. 1923 (2026-10-30): R.C. 1923.12 to 1923.14's storage, notice, valuation, sale and title machinery is park-only on its own face, and R.C. 1923.13(A)'s general writ says nothing about belongings. Its own flagged post-writ PET gap is confirmed again and is now carried by this row.
- (`property-tax-rent-disclosure`) Points to `tax-escalation-oh` (being written by the fees batch in this pass) and to the existing `edu-rent-increase-notice-gap-oh`. No duplication: this row's subject is the statement, theirs is the escalation and the notice period.
- (`religious-cultural-display`) Same gap as recorded under `political-access`: `edu-flag-display-oh` carries R.C. 5321.131(A) and (B) but not (C) or (D). Division (D) - the only statutory per-se unconscionable lease term in Ohio - and division (C) - which relieves a landlord who received no (B) notification from 'any damages, fines, or costs associated with any issues arising from the placement of the flag pole or the bracket by the tenant' - are both absent from it. Reported once in each row rather than edited, per the briefing. No error found in its existing text.
- (`rent-demand-bar`) The shared `rent-payment` clause's 'without demand, deduction, or setoff' language is load-bearing in Ohio rather than boilerplate: Ohio has no statute making rent payable in advance or without demand ('without demand' and 'payable in advance' both return 0 corpus sections), so the clause supplies what the statute does not. Separately, R.C. 1923.02(A)(2) reaches nonpayment only under an ORAL tenancy, so a written-lease nonpayment case runs on (A)(9) and depends on the lease expressly imposing the rent obligation. Both points are worth adding to that clause's OH note at the next edit pass; no correction needed.
- (`rent-reporting`) edu-tenant-screening-oh (written this pass, B07-dv-protected) says Ohio barely regulates screening and hands the landlord two liability shields. One addition, read section-open: R.C. 1349.52(L)(3)(e) excuses a consumer reporting agency from placing a security freeze on a database that 'consists entirely of consumer information concerning, and used solely for ... Tenant screening', and R.C. 1349.52(A)(8) defines the freeze itself as a restriction on releasing a report 'relating to the extension of credit'. So an Ohio security freeze is not a reason a screening report comes back empty, which is a thing landlords are told and get wrong. Also worth adding there: R.C. 1349.17's consent condition on recording an applicant's social security number in connection with a payment. Not edited (briefing rule 3).
- (`renters-insurance-rules`) {
 "id": "tenants-property-insurance-ks-oh-ca",
 "finding": "Not an error - a confirmation, plus one thing worth adding. The row's merge notes record that Ohio reached the shared text 'by deleting the same liability-limiting sentence under its own exculpation ban (ORC 5321.13(D))'. I read R.C. 5321.13 whole section-open and that is right: the clause as it stands requires the tenant to insure the tenant's own property and liability and to show evidence of coverage, which R.C. 5321.06 permits, and contains nothing that exculpates or indemnifies the landlord. The 2026-09-19 reconciliation of rule_type from REQUIRED to RECOMMENDED is also right for Ohio - no Ohio statute mandates a renter's-insurance clause. The one thing the clause does NOT do, and which an Ohio landlord usually wants, is get notice if the policy lapses. Adding a request to be named as an interested party or certificate holder (as pet-insurance-requirement already does) would serve the landlord without running into R.C. 5321.13(D), whereas asking to be an additional insured would. I have not edited the row."
}
- (`sale-or-management-change`) {
 "id": "edu-nuisance-receivership-oh",
 "finding": "Not an error - an addition its own notes invite. That row says 'NOT READ: the procedural detail of 3767.41 (notice, hearing, who may be appointed, how the receivership ends)'. I have now read R.C. 3767.41 whole. Two facts the row does not carry and which matter to a landlord: division (F)(1) empowers the receiver to 'establish and collect rents and income, lease and rent the building and the property, and evict tenants' - so Ohio gives a sitting residential tenant no protection from a receiver, unlike the states whose statutes bar a receiver from rejecting a lease of a tenant's primary residence; and division (B)(1)(b) requires sixty days' pre-suit written notice to the landlord before an action about SUBSIDIZED housing, with (B)(2)(c) deeming the property not a public nuisance where HUD's real estate assessment center scored it 75 or higher out of 100 in the preceding twelve months and conditions have not significantly changed. I have not edited the row; the receivership procedure is carried in full by the new edu-substandard-property-receivership-oh in this batch."
}
- (`scope`) Not an existing row but a SHARED-FILE error with wide reach. OHIO_FRAMING.md section 4 and /home/claude/corpus/README.txt both gloss R.C. 5321.01(C)(7) as the manufactured-home-park exclusion. The section's own text excludes 'Dwelling units subject to Chapter 3733. of the Revised Code', and chapter 3733 is AGRICULTURAL LABOR CAMPS (R.C. 3733.41 to 3733.99). The enrolled text of Am. Sub. H.B. 33, 135th G.A., held in the corpus, shows the limb amended from 'sections 3733.41 to 3733.49' to 'Chapter 3733.', so it pointed at the labour-camp sections before the amendment too and has never been the park exclusion. Park lots are outside chapter 5321 because R.C. 4781.01(W) makes a park's 'residential premises' the LOT while R.C. 5321.01(C) requires a dwelling unit, and because R.C. 4781.36 to 4781.52 is a parallel code. Twenty-four saved answers from B02 to B13 cite (C)(7) for the park point: B02 confirmed-absences-habitability; B04 fee-transparency, required-fees; B05 meter-conservation-charge, utility-disclosure-attachment; B06 flood-disclosure; B08 common-area-lighting, health-district-rental-rules, pool-safety; B09 abandonment-and-mitigation, extended-absence-notice, owner-move-in-reservation, term-change-notice; B10 confession-of-judgment, exculpatory-clauses, lease-type-parity, optional-lease-terms, part5-nonwaivable, unconscionability; B11 rules-regulations; B12 emergency-contact, tenant-right-to-organize; B13 foreclosure-disclosure, sale-or-management-change. Their CONCLUSIONS survive; only the citation needs replacing. I corrected the five of my own B14 files that had copied it.

- (`showing-notice-waiver`) DO NOT EXTEND `showing-notice-la` TO OHIO. That shared clause (states LA;AR;MS;VI;AS;MP;CO;WY;KS) allows showings in the last part of the term 'on [state the shorter notice, for example same-day notice by telephone or text message] instead of the notice required by the Landlord's Right of Entry section'. In Ohio that is an attempt to modify R.C. 5321.04(A)(8) by agreement, which R.C. 5321.13(A) does not recognise, so the clause would promise the landlord a notice period the statute will not support. Its sibling notes confirm it was built for states with no entry statute. The shared `landlords-access` row, which does carry OH, already states the 24-hour notice correctly and should remain the only Ohio showing-notice term.
- (`sprinkler-disclosure`) No conflict with `edu-alarm-duty-fire-code-oh` (topic_key `alarm-duties`, 'Ohio's Alarm Duty Comes From Code - Except in a High-Rise'), which holds the smoke/CO alarm side of R.C. 3781.104. This row takes the sprinkler-and-disclosure side only and does not restate the alarm duty (rule 57). The two rows agree on the seventy-five-foot limit.
- (`statutory-early-termination`) edu-no-servicemember-lease-rule-oh ('Ohio Has No Servicemember Residential Lease Termination') is correct as far as STATE law goes, but R.C. 5321.04(A)(10), read section-open, makes it an Ohio landlord duty to 'Comply with the rights of tenants under the Servicemembers Civil Relief Act, 117 Stat. 2835, 50 U.S.C. App. 501'. So Ohio has no termination right of its own and does make the federal one a state-law duty. I did not edit that row; the point is carried in this row's FEDERAL limb and the lead may want to add an OH note there. Divides from: edu-habitability-escrow-oh (the R.C. 5321.07 escrow route, not the (B)(3) termination limb); repair-escrow-exemption-notice-oh (the clause that buys the R.C. 5321.07(C) escape); edu-no-dv-lease-termination-oh; landlords-access and edu-tenant-cure-period-oh; and edu-retaliation-oh, which carries the R.C. 5321.02 triggers and remedies - I cite R.C. 5321.02(B)(3) only as the third statutory exit, which is the limb this topic is about. I read edu-retaliation-oh in full: it is CORRECT and it already states the termination remedy, so there is no gap and no overlap worth worrying about - this row lists the exit, that row explains the triggers and the costs. ONE FLAG ON THAT ROW IS NOW CLOSED: its notes say 'R.C. 5321.03 lets a landlord still bring a possession action on the grounds listed there notwithstanding 5321.02 - NOT read section-open in this pass, flagged.' I READ R.C. 5321.03 SECTION-OPEN on 2026-10-30 (eff. 2023-10-03, Latest Legislation H.B. 33, 135th G.A.) and it bears out what that row says: '(A) Notwithstanding section 5321.02 of the Revised Code, a landlord may bring an action under Chapter 1923. of the Revised Code for possession of the premises if: (1) The tenant is in default in the payment of rent; (2) The violation ... was primarily caused by any act or lack of reasonable care by the tenant ...; (3) Compliance with the applicable building, housing, health, or safety code would require alteration, remodeling, or demolition of the premises which would effectively deprive the tenant of the use of the dwelling unit; (4) A tenant is holding over the tenant's term.' plus (A)(5), the registered-offender ground, with (B) preserving the tenant's damages claim and (C) excluding a student tenant. The lead may want to clear that flag on edu-retaliation-oh.
- (`student-housing`) edu-scope-oh (written this pass, B14-content) is titled 'Who Ohio's Landlord-Tenant Law Covers - Ten Exclusions, Two Section-Level Carve-Outs, and a Hotel Problem' and its body names only R.C. 5321.07(C) and 5321.07(D) as the carve-outs inside the chapter. There are TWO MORE that bear on the same student-tenant question and are not in it: R.C. 5321.03(C) 'This section does not apply to a dwelling unit occupied by a student tenant' (so the landlord's possession grounds in that section are withdrawn too), and R.C. 1923.02(D), which takes the whole forcible-entry-and-detainer chapter away 'when the college or university proceeds to terminate a rental agreement pursuant to section 5321.031'. Nothing in edu-scope-oh is incorrect; the count in its title and the list in its body are incomplete. Not edited (briefing rule 3).
- (`substandard-property-receivership`) {
 "id": "edu-nuisance-receivership-oh",
 "finding": "Not an error - its own stated gap, now filled. Its notes say 'NOT READ: the procedural detail of 3767.41 (notice, hearing, who may be appointed, how the receivership ends) and the related nuisance sections in ch. 3767.' I have read R.C. 3767.41 whole (33,868 characters) and the procedure is now carried by the new edu-substandard-property-receivership-oh. Three corrections of emphasis worth recording against the existing row's body, none of which makes it wrong: (1) it says 'If you have refused or failed to abate, the court may appoint a receiver' - R.C. 3767.41(C)(2) inserts a mandatory step before that, requiring the judge to offer mortgagees, lienholders and other interested parties, in order of priority of title, the opportunity to do the work, and a receiver may be appointed only if none is willing or able or one fails to proceed with due diligence; (2) its 'the property can be sold to satisfy them' understates (J)(2), under which the judge may order a sale if the receivership's costs are unpaid three days after a declaration that the nuisance has been abated; (3) under (K) the purchaser's title is clear only of unsatisfiable STATE and political-subdivision tax liens - all other liens, a properly filed federal tax lien and recorded easements and covenants survive the sale. I have not edited the row."
}
