- (`tenant-death`) I considered tagging tenant-death-contact-nh (LEASE_CLAUSE, SERVES_LANDLORD, used in NH;ME;VT;AK;AR;MS;GU;VI;AS;MP) for Ohio and DECIDED AGAINST IT for a specific, checkable reason: its text says Landlord 'will handle any property left at the property under this Lease's Handling of Property Left Behind Section and applicable law', and every existing tag note answers that pointer with that state's own abandoned-property clause (abandoned-property-me, -vt, -ak, -ar, -ms, -gu). OHIO HAS NO SUCH CLAUSE - Ohio's only abandoned-property row is edu-no-abandoned-property-safe-harbor-oh, an education row recording that Ohio gives a landlord no disposal procedure at all. Worth noting in the row for future states: in Ohio the clause CREATES the duty rather than restating one, which is the opposite of its California origin, and breach of it attracts no statutory damages or fees.
- (`tenant-records`) {
 "id": "Ohio Adm.Code 4112-5-04(B) - a stale cross-reference in the rule itself, not an error in any library row",
 "finding": "Recorded because two Ohio rows now rest on this rule and the lead should know the cross-reference is off. Ohio Adm.Code 4112-5-04(B) (read whole from the corpus, eff. 2023-04-11) says that making and maintaining records of a housing applicant's protected characteristics 'shall not violate division (H)(7) of section 4112.02 of the Revised Code' where made to a government agency's or court's requirements. But R.C. 4112.02's current divisions, read section-open this session (eff. 2023-04-06, H.B. 281, 134th G.A.), put the inquiry-and-record ban at (H)(8) - 'make any inquiry, elicit any information, make or keep any record, or use any form of application containing questions or entries concerning race, color, religion, sex, military status, familial status, ancestry, disability, or national origin' - while (H)(7) is the advertising limb, about printing, publishing or circulating a statement indicating a preference. So the rule's safe harbour points at the wrong division of the statute. The B07 agent read (H)(8) correctly and 'edu-protected-class-inquiry-ban-oh' states it correctly; the mismatch is in the Administrative Code, not in any row. A related oddity in the same place, also unresolved: R.C. 4112.02(H)(8) opens 'Except as otherwise provided in division (H)(8) or (17) of this section' - a division excepting itself, which reads like the same renumbering artefact. I did NOT reconcile either; both look like consequences of the statute being renumbered without the rule following. Practical effect for a landlord is nil, because the rule's substance plainly addresses record keeping and the statute's record-keeping ban carries its own monitoring-compliance exception in the same division. No row needs changing."
}
- (`tenant-repair-remedies`) edu-habitability-escrow-oh (topic_key landlord-maintenance, LANDLORD_EDUCATION, OH) already answers this topic in full and no new row should be written. Its body states the R.C. 5321.07(A) written notice and the address, the cure window ('within a reasonable time considering the severity, or within thirty days, whichever is sooner'), the current-in-rent precondition, all three of R.C. 5321.07(B)'s options (deposit all rent with the clerk of the municipal or county court; apply to the court for an order to remedy, including an order reducing the rent until the landlord does or an order spending the deposited rent on the repair; terminate the rental agreement), and both carve-outs - R.C. 5321.07(C) three or fewer dwelling units with notice of that fact, and R.C. 5321.07(D) student tenants. I re-read R.C. 5321.07 section-open on 2026-10-30 and the row is accurate as written; its Effective and Latest Legislation line is October 12, 1994, House Bill 438 - 120th General Assembly, which the row does not record, so the one improvement available is adding that line to its notes. Two companion sections the row's own notes flag as NOT read section-open - R.C. 5321.09 (landlord application for release of rent) and R.C. 5321.10 (release of part of rent) - I DID read section-open this session, and the flag can now be lifted: 5321.09 gives the landlord four release grounds (the condition has been remedied, with the clerk releasing on the tenant's written confirmation; defective 5321.07(A) notice; tenant not current in rent when deposits began; no violation at all), requires the tenant to be named with a right to answer and counterclaim, requires trial within sixty days absent good cause, and makes the tenant liable for the landlord's damages and costs, plus fees for intentional bad faith, where the condition was the tenant's own act or omission; 5321.10 lets the court release part of the deposited rent during the action for mortgage interest and principal, insurance, real estate taxes, utilities, repairs and other customary operating costs, weighing the rent from other units in the building, the cost of operating them and the cost of remedying the condition. That is the landlord-facing half the escrow row says it lacks, and it belongs in a separate row rather than as an edit - see question_for_taylor.
- (`tenant-right-to-organize`) `edu-retaliation-oh` is CORRECT as written - its three triggers match R.C. 5321.02(A)(1) to (3) read section-open, including the organising trigger, and its closed-list caution is right. Two notes for the lead, neither an error in the body: (1) its notes flag R.C. 5321.03 as 'NOT read section-open in this pass' - it has now been read whole and has FIVE grounds, (A)(1) to (A)(5), the fifth being the registered-offender ground added by H.B. 33, 135th G.A.; the batch brief for this batch repeats the 'four grounds' figure and is also wrong. (2) The row does not say that non-renewal is absent from R.C. 5321.02(A)'s list of retaliatory acts while present in the otherwise identical park provision R.C. 4781.36(A); that comparison is new and now sits in this row.
- (`term-change-notice`) Divides from edu-rent-increase-notice-gap-oh (topic_key rent-increase-notice), which carries the absence of a rent-increase notice period. This row is about changing non-rent terms, rules and fees and the mechanism (agreement, or termination plus re-letting); it does not restate the rent-increase point as its own.
- (`trespass-notice`) `edu-self-help-eviction-ban-oh` (self-help-eviction, PROHIBITED, OH) is titled 'Never Shut Off Utilities Or Change The Locks In Ohio' and is the adjacent row. Nothing in it is wrong, but two things in R.C. 5321.15 as I read it section-open are worth checking against its body if the lead has it to hand: the protection expressly extends to 'a tenant whose right to possession has terminated', and (A) also reaches 'exclusion from the premises, or threat of any unlawful act' - not only utilities and locks. If that row stops at utilities and locks it understates the section, and the former-tenant limb is the single most useful words in it for the trespass question. I have not edited it.
- (`unconscionability`) Not an error, but worth the lead's note: `edu-flag-display-oh` states R.C. 5321.131's prohibition without its (D) consequence - that a violating lease provision is 'against public policy and unenforceable' and is deemed 'an unconscionable term under section 5321.14'. The new `edu-unconscionability-oh` carries that limb; the flag row needs no edit.
- (`veterans-incentive`) edu-no-servicemember-lease-rule-oh ('Ohio Has No Servicemember Residential Lease Termination'). The pass has already logged twice that it omits R.C. 5321.04(A)(10), so that is not re-reported. TWO ADDITIONS from this batch, both read section-open. (1) Ohio DID write active-duty termination rights - for other contracts: R.C. 1349.02 lets a person deployed on active duty for not less than one hundred eighty days terminate a motor vehicle lease entered into on or after that section's effective date, and R.C. 1349.03 does the same for a cellular phone contract. The absence of a housing counterpart is therefore a deliberate-looking gap rather than an oversight, which makes the existing row's conclusion stronger and worth saying that way. (2) R.C. 1349.04(B) directs the Attorney General to staff expedited handling of active-duty cases and lists the sections it covers - including R.C. 1923.062, 4933.12 and 4933.121 but NOT any section of chapter 5321 - which is independent confirmation of the same gap. Not edited (briefing rule 3).
- (`written-notice-required`) `edu-termination-notice-periods-oh`'s note that R.C. 5321.17 does not require the notice to be in writing is CONFIRMED on an independent section-open read 2026-10-30, and now extends to (C): even the mandatory drug-activity termination notice is framed as 'giving a notice of termination' with no written form prescribed. No correction needed. Separately, R.C. 1923.02(A)(9) is limited on its face to 'an obligation imposed upon them by a written rental agreement', so an oral Ohio tenancy has no general lease-breach eviction ground - worth adding to `edu-tenant-cure-period-oh`'s Track Two discussion, which currently assumes a written lease without saying that the writing is what creates the ground.

### §7 Findings for other states

- **`hoa-compliance` is shared across 50 states.** Ohio's conflict (§4 item 3) turns on a condominium statute assessing enforcement costs to the **owner**, which most states have in some form. Worth asking wherever a condominium or planned-community statute levies on the owner or the lot and the state also voids tenant indemnities of the landlord's statutory liability. Not tested outside Ohio.

- **`tenant-caused-fines-as` (shared: AS;MP;WY;MN;NE) has the same shape as the `hoa-compliance` defect, and should be looked at before OH or any similar state is ever added.** It makes the tenant 'reimburse Landlord the amount Landlord paid' for a government fine or a nuisance-abatement charge levied on the landlord. Where the statute levies on the OWNER and the state also refuses to recognise a tenant's agreement to indemnify the landlord for a liability arising under law - which is what R.C. 5321.13(D) does in Ohio - that is an indemnity of the landlord's own statutory liability, and the lawful route is the same one proposed for `hoa-compliance`: recover it as damages for the tenant's breach of the lease rather than as reimbursement. Its existing carve-out ('a condition Landlord is responsible for under this Lease or the law') does not reach the point, because the landlord IS responsible for the fine under the law even when the tenant caused the condition. Ohio was not tagged into this row and the row was not re-audited; flagged for the five states it does carry.

- **`edu-waiver-by-acceptance-oh` is sound but rests entirely on case law, which is worth a library-level decision rather than a state one.** Its notes cite five appellate decisions and label the row 'CASE LAW, not statute (Instructions item 16)', while SOP 1.88 rule 1 says cases are leads only and never load-bearing. Nothing in the 37 held chapters addresses acceptance of rent - 'acceptance of rent' returns zero sections - so there is no statutory route to the proposition, and two new rows in this delta had to point at it rather than restate it. Either rule 1 has an exception for a settled multi-district consensus or this row needs re-basing; that is a question for Taylor and the SOP, not for Ohio.

- **The non-waiver clause's scope is worth checking in every state.** Ohio's
R.C. 5321.13(A) says "no provision of **this chapter**" — so it reaches the landlord-tenant chapter's
own notice provisions but **not** the separate eviction chapter's three-day notice in
R.C. 1923.04(A), and chapter 1923 has no non-waiver clause of its own. Any state whose eviction
procedure lives in a different chapter from its landlord-tenant act may have the same gap, and a row
asserting an eviction notice cannot be waived may rest on cases about something else — here,
acceptance of rent *after* the notice rather than a pre-dispute waiver. Not tested outside Ohio.
- **A statute's catchline is not its scope, and two Ohio sections prove it.** R.C. 3781.104 is
catchlined "Smoke detector systems or sprinkler system for apartment buildings and condominiums" but
reaches only buildings over seventy-five feet and has no carbon-monoxide provision at all;
R.C. 3742.41 is catchlined "Presumptions relative to property constructed prior to 1-1-78" and
contains no presumption. Both would mislead anyone working from a title index. Worth a spot-check
wherever a row's only citation is to a section nobody opened.
- **Interest-on-unpaid-amounts rows may import a ceiling that does not apply.** Ohio's eight per cent
in R.C. 1343.01(A) governs "a bond, bill, promissory note, or other instrument of writing for the
forbearance or payment of money at any future time", and whether a residential lease is one of those
is unsettled on the text. Several sibling rows state a state's usury ceiling as though it plainly
bound a lease. Worth asking in each.
- **A municipal utility lien may be far narrower than the sibling rows assume.** Ohio's
R.C. 743.04(A)(1)(a) permits certification to the tax list only where the charges arose under a
service contract made directly with an **owner who occupies** the property, which closes the lien
route on rentals entirely. If other states carry the same owner-occupant proviso, any row telling
landlords that a tenant's unpaid city water bill becomes their lien is overstated. Not tested
outside Ohio.
- **Biometric smart-access deserves a per-state check.** Ohio has no biometric privacy statute and
R.C. 1349.19(A)(7)(a)'s "personal information" excludes a biometric template, so an Ohio landlord
holding fingerprint or face data has no breach-notification duty for it. Illinois, Texas and
Washington do have such statutes, so a shared smart-access row written from Ohio's position would be
wrong in those states.


### Proposed SOP changes

1. **The Ohio kickoff template names a chapter that no longer exists.** Ohio item 4 asks the pass to
read "chapter 3796 (medical marijuana) and R.C. 3780 (adult-use cannabis, 2023; fetch both)". There
is one chapter: ch. 3780 was amended and renumbered into ch. 3796 by S.B. 56, 136th G.A., effective
2026-03-20, and ch. 3796 now holds both programs. Correct the template, and treat this as the second
instance of a renumbering wave (R.C. 955.011 to 955.021 by H.B. 247 was the first) rather than a
one-off: a kickoff template that names chapters by number goes stale, so the template should say to
confirm the chapter still exists before relying on the number.

2. **Rule 80's whole-section requirement should extend to the tool's own construction, not only its
output.** The requirement as written is that the printer must print the whole section. The failure
this pass actually hit was upstream of that: a strip regex written to remove the site's
"Download Authenticated PDF" furniture deleted everything from that marker to the end of the string,
which is the entire body, and the printer still returned a plausible-looking result — the heading and
the Effective and Latest Legislation lines. The row of a section read that way would have carried a
correct citation and no content. Suggested addition: a checker's section printer must be validated
against the authenticated PDF of at least one long section before any agent uses it, and must report
a character count with each section so a truncation is visible rather than silent.

3. **A batch brief's claim about the corpus must be generated, not written.** Twice this pass my own
batch brief told an agent a chapter was in the corpus when it was not — ch. 1319 to the fees batch,
chs. 2505 and 2727 to the eviction batch. Both agents caught it and reported the discrepancy, so the
error cost two `NOT_LOCATED` verdicts and no wrong law. But a brief that misstates the corpus can
also produce the opposite failure, where an agent stops searching a chapter it was told was absent.
Suggested addition: the lead must generate the held-chapter list from the corpus index and hand it to
every agent as a file, and the standing briefing must tell agents that where a brief disagrees with
the corpus, the corpus wins — flag it and carry on. Done this pass (`HELD_CHAPTERS.txt`), and worth
making standing.

4. **An agent's NOT_LOCATED should be a request, not a verdict, while a fetch route exists.** Rule 36
correctly separates "not located" from "confirmed absent", and a canvass agent with no network is
right to stop rather than guess. But the verdict as saved reads as a finding about Ohio law, when it
is a fact about the agent's reach: one of the two verdicts this pass received was removed within the
hour by the lead fetching five sections. Suggested wording: where the pass has any fetch route, an
agent's NOT_LOCATED is provisional and the lead must either fetch the chapter or record in the log why
it could not be fetched, before the verdict is delivered.

5. **Every battery should be machine-re-run before a pass is delivered.** Writing one row I recorded
hit counts from memory instead of running them, and four of the five were wrong. Nothing in the SOP
would have caught it: a battery is self-reported, and a row carrying an unrun battery is
unfalsifiable — which is the opposite of what rule 19 is for. I wrote `verify_batteries.py`, which
re-runs every term in every saved battery against the corpus and reports each count that does not
reproduce, distinguishing a legitimately scoped search (over the OAC, the Fire Code rule PDFs, or
enrolled act text) from a count that simply was not run. Over 121 answers it re-ran 353 terms and
surfaced six real errors across five rows, and a seventh on a later run. Suggested addition to rule
19 or rule 80: a pass must re-run its own batteries mechanically before delivery, and the log must
report the number of terms re-run and every discrepancy with its disposition.

**The requirement should bind the canvass agent too, and there is now evidence it works.** Told about
the lead's error and the verifier, the termination-batch agent wrote **its own** re-run script before
saving, caught and fixed **two of its own wrong counts** unprompted, and returned a batch with zero
mismatches. Pushing the check down to the agent is cheaper than catching it at the top and it
produced a visibly better batch, so the rule should require the agent to verify its own battery
before saving a topic, with the lead's machine re-run as the backstop rather than the first line.

6. **A corrective edit must be swept exhaustively and re-verified, not phrase-matched.** One error in
this pass — R.C. 5321.01(C)(7) glossed as the manufactured-home-park exclusion — took **three**
correction passes. The first matched a handful of phrasings and left nineteen rows stale, one of them
carrying a correction note its own body contradicted. The second, written after the independent check
caught that, fixed fourteen rows and still missed four, because it matched the phrasings it had seen
and not the verbs "keeps" and "puts". Only the third worked, and it worked because the method changed:
every occurrence of the literal string was enumerated and classified by hand, and the enumeration was
re-run afterwards to prove nothing remained. Suggested addition: when a pass corrects a proposition
that more than one row carries, it must (a) enumerate every occurrence of the citation or phrase
across the delta, bodies, notes and tag notes alike, (b) classify each one rather than substitute
blindly, and (c) re-run the enumeration and record the count in the log. Ordinary sentence splitting
is not enough for a statutory citation, because a citation like `5321.01(C)(7)` contains periods.

7. **The two-round cap on rule 80 should bite on a round that finds nothing, not on a round number.**
Both rounds of the check this pass found errors, and round 2's most serious findings were partly
*introduced* by round 1's fixes: a hedge left standing beside the paragraph that resolved it, a
corrected row contradicting another row that had always been right, and round 1's own bound-in-body
correction applied to nine rows in a set and not to three others. The cap then stopped the check while
it was still finding things, and twenty rows changed after the last round went out unchecked. The rule
should read: the check stops after a round that produces no findings, or after a stated maximum, and
where the maximum is reached with findings still coming, the log must list the rows changed after the
last round and say plainly that they are uncertified.

8. **A pass needs one cross-row consistency check, because per-topic research cannot catch a
contradiction between two rows.** The sharpest error this pass is a good example: one row said
"nothing in Ohio's landlord-tenant or eviction chapters sets a deadline for suing" while another,
written in a different batch, correctly cited R.C. 1923.01(B)'s two-year limit on an eviction action.
Both rows were right against their own search; the delta was wrong as a whole. Nothing in the workflow
compares rows to each other. The same shape produced round 2's finding 1 (two rows disagreeing on
R.C. 5321.04(A)(6)'s exception) and finding 5 (two rows disagreeing on whether R.C. 5321.02(B)'s
remedies are alternatives). Suggested addition: before delivery, a pass must run one pass over the
delta grouping rows by the sections they cite and reading the groups where two rows describe the same
provision — which is cheap, because the citation is already in every row's notes.

### Proposed topic questions

1. **Escrow release has no topic_key.** `edu-habitability-escrow-oh` tells an Ohio landlord the tenant
can deposit rent with the clerk, and its own notes flag that R.C. 5321.09 and 5321.10 "are what a
landlord actually needs to get the money out" and were never read section-open. They have now been
read section-open in this pass, and they hold: four grounds for release, release by the clerk on the
tenant's written confirmation, a sixty-day trial setting, the tenant's liability for a bad-faith
deposit, and a partial-release list covering mortgage interest and principal, insurance, taxes,
utilities and repairs. None of it has anywhere to go, because no topic in the library asks the
question. Suggested topic: **`escrow-release`** — how a landlord recovers rent a tenant has deposited
with the court. The content is in hand and the row could be written immediately.

2. **Pre-lease money has no home.** `holding-deposit` asks about money taken before a lease exists.
Rule 55 yields no clause, because the lease is not the vehicle, so the topic can only ever produce an
education row and the landlord is left with nothing to use — in Ohio, where R.C. 5321.01(E) defines a
security deposit as 'any deposit of money or property to secure performance', that is the one place in
the deposit family where the landlord most needs a form. Suggested topic question: should the library
carry pre-lease instruments (a holding-deposit receipt, an application-fee receipt) as a document type
of their own, or should these topics be marked education-only by design so a pass does not keep
rediscovering the gap?

3. **Two propositions the Ohio set can only point at, not carry, and both are library-level.** First,
the **Fair Credit Reporting Act** (15 U.S.C. 1681) is the regime that actually governs tenant
screening, the adverse-action notice and the use of a consumer reporting agency. Three rows in this
delta gesture at it and no state appears to carry a row on it. Should it be one shared `FEDERAL:` row
or a sentence per state? The same question arises for a shared **HUD/HAP** row behind the three
subsidy topics. Second, **waiver by acceptance of rent** exists in Ohio only in appellate case law:
`ohsearch find "acceptance of rent"` returns **zero** sections across the 37 held chapters, and the
existing `edu-waiver-by-acceptance-oh` rests on five decisions while rule 1 says cases are leads only
and never load-bearing. Two rows in this delta had to point at it rather than restate it. Either
rule 1 needs a stated exception for a settled multi-district consensus, or that row needs re-basing —
and whichever it is, the answer governs every state, not Ohio.

4. **A variable-naming convention is missing, and this pass had to invent one.** `student-housing-oh`
needs to carry the identifiers R.C. 5321.031 requires a college or university letting to its own
student tenant to put in the agreement, two of which are pointers to the institution's own documents.
There is no existing family for that: the library has `{{myhazards_url}}` and
`{{payment_portal_url}}` and no variable anywhere containing conduct, handbook, policy or code. This
pass used `{{conduct_code_reference}}` and `{{hearing_procedures_reference}}`, opening a `_reference`
suffix with no precedent. If the intended family is `_url`, or something else, these should be renamed
before sync and the convention written down, because every state with a student-housing statute will
hit the same question.

5. **One topic is arguably a methodology row wearing a state row's clothes.** A batch agent observed
that `tpa-sunset` is really asking how to tell whether the law a clause relies on has changed — which
is a question about the library's own maintenance, not about any state's statute. Worth deciding
whether it belongs in the topic list at all, or in the SOP.

6. **A topic for R.C. 2953.26, the certificate of qualification for housing.** It is currently a
paragraph inside `edu-tenant-screening-oh`, and it is too substantial for that: it lifts an automatic
conviction-based housing bar, creates a rebuttable presumption of fitness, preserves the landlord's
case-by-case judgment, and — the part landlords read backwards — **gives the landlord immunity in a
negligent-leasing tort action** where the landlord knew of the certificate (division (F)(2)), with
"decision-maker" expressly defined to include a landlord. Other states have analogous
certificate-of-relief regimes, so this is likely a shared topic rather than an Ohio one.

## Gaps-pass sync review (Claude Code, 2026-10-30)

Merged with `merge-delta.py --base c05dc07`: 260 new rows (37 lease clauses) and 8 shared rows tagged, nothing refused. All 257 missing topics are answered. Desktop marked four topics as already answered by rows keyed to other topics (`confession-of-judgment`, `exculpatory-clauses`, `part5-nonwaivable`, `tenant-repair-remedies`). Each now has its own row: `edu-confession-of-judgment-oh`, `edu-exculpatory-clauses-oh` and `edu-part5-nonwaivable-oh` (R.C. 5321.13, 5321.14, 4781.47) point to the existing rows, and `edu-tenant-repair-remedies-oh` carries R.C. 5321.07-5321.10 read whole, including the release of escrowed rent. Ohio now carries 385 active rows, every one VERIFIED; the new rows carry the canvass date in `effective_from` (SOP rule 70).

**NEEDS_REVIEW (14 delivered), all closed VERIFIED**, read on codes.ohio.gov (Desktop's container couldn't reach it), the Ohio Civil Rules, eCFR and govinfo. Corrections:
- **Pools:** the standards are rules under R.C. 3749.02, not R.C. 3749.04 (the licence duty); Ohio's barrier figures now in the row (Ohio Adm.Code 3701-31-05.1(O)).
- **Fee in lieu of deposit:** soliciting a particular insurer's product needs a licence (R.C. 3905.01-.03); no landlord exception.
- **Lead:** the registry rules set no registration procedure, so the cautious course is to ask the Department of Health.
- **Trespass:** R.C. 2911.21's notice limbs; the occupying-claimant protection doesn't reach a squatter.
- **Jury waiver:** Civ.R. 38-39 recognise only a missed demand or a stipulation in a pending case.
- `edu-scope-oh`: a miscount.
- Doubts only case law could settle are stated with the cautious course.

**Findings on existing rows (§6) and the items:** checked against the statute; 32 edits and three overrides:
- `hoa-compliance-oh` and `tenant-caused-fines-oh`: Ohio levies association and code fines on the owner (R.C. 5311.19(B)(3), 5312.11(A)) and won't recognise a tenant's indemnity of the landlord's statutory liability (R.C. 5321.13(D)), so both recover the fine as damages for the tenant's breach, without attorney's fees (R.C. 5321.13(C)).
- `early-termination-oh`: a flat one month's Rent superseding `early-termination-ks`'s greater-of-30%-of-remaining-Rent fee.
- `edu-mandatory-drug-termination-oh`: the trigger is defined by R.C. 1923.02(A)(6)(a)(i) (the search-warrant chain plus an officer's notice).
- `edu-alarm-duty-fire-code-oh`: the Residential Code's smoke and CO alarm rules, read (Ohio Adm.Code 4101:8-3-01).
- `edu-nuisance-receivership-oh`: the mandatory offer to lienholders before a receiver.
- `edu-portfolio-thresholds-oh`: seven thresholds, not five.
- R.C. 5321.03 has five grounds, not four.
- Two rows pointed to renamed sibling rows; fixed.
- OH tagged on `furnishings-included-la`.

**Staging errors of mine, caught by the pass:** the prompt's item 4 named ch. 3780, which S.B. 56 (136th G.A., eff. 2026-03-20) folded into ch. 3796, as Ohio's own retro recorded; and the corpus README described R.C. 5321.01(C)(7) as the manufactured-home-park exclusion, when it excludes "Dwelling units subject to Chapter 3733." (now agricultural labor camps only). The README is corrected, `edu-seasonal-tenancy-oh` was the one row still carrying the error, and SOP 1.91 rule 24 now has staged items and README descriptions checked against the corpus and the state's log. Currency: R.C. 2305.06 (S.B. 219, eff. 2026-09-23) is already cited correctly; every ch. 3780 mention names it as the former number.

**Edited-after rows:** the 22 rows listed after Desktop's last round (one was NEEDS_REVIEW and closed above) were read; 9 fixes:
- R.C. 1923.02(C)(1)'s premises list;
- "owned or operated" in R.C. 5321.01(H);
- R.C. 5321.02(B)'s remedies are alternatives;
- no fee award under R.C. 5321.07 or 5321.12;
- the ten-day utility-transfer liability (Ohio Adm.Code 4901:1-18-08(K));
- an unsettled point stated as settled;
- an over-broad absence claim narrowed to the chapters searched.

`edu-waiver-by-acceptance-oh` now presents the waiver-by-acceptance rule as the courts' view (no statute addresses it). `lease-copy-receipt-oh` was rewritten from "you/we" into Landlord/Tenant. No new clause has guidance outside brackets.

**Spot check:** deposits (R.C. 5321.16), entry (5321.04(A)(8), 5321.05(B)), landlord duties and nonwaiver (5321.04, 5321.13), tenant remedies and escrow (5321.07-5321.10), retaliation (5321.02) and eviction notices (1923.04, 5321.11, 5321.17) across all Ohio rows. Fixes:
- the forwarding-address cite (5321.16(B));
- the deposit-pledge rule credited to the courts;
- R.C. 5321.09(D)'s tenant liability added where a row denied any;
- the three-day notice is 1923.04(A);
- a written-lease breach does carry R.C. 5321.12 damages and a 1923.02(A)(9) possession ground.

**Decisions (§4):** the log lists 53 points Desktop settled itself (rule 76) without asking Taylor. Legal calls agreed here: no arbitration clause, no jury-waiver clause, and `tax-escalation-oh` offered as drafted. The four product points (shared federal rows for PTFA, FCRA and HAP tenancies; a county attribute for ch. 5323; `student-housing-oh` for institutions only; Steinoak's own R.C. 1349.19 breach process) are in the backlog with recommendations.

**Other:**
- Six new `{{variables}}` to M.14.
- SOP 1.91 takes up the pass's 8 proposals as 4 grouped additions, all [Fwd].
- Three topic questions added under existing keys.
- The citations file gained 275 rows (the extractor gained an Ohio pattern).
- The two-round cap Ohio hit is the gaps template's (Taylor's usage budget), not the SOP's; unchanged.
